\mathbf{F}=\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{(p+q)\times(p+q)},

where

\mathbf{A}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{U}\\
\mathbf{V}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{(p+q)\times r}.

The initialization is based on a Fantope projection problem, which solves

\displaystyle\min_{\mathbf{F}\in\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle-\langle\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}},\mathbf{F}\rangle+\rho\|\mathbf{F}\|_{1}\quad\text{subject to}\quad

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{0}^{1/2}\mathbf{F}\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{0}^{1/2}\in\mathcal{F},

where

\mathcal{F}=\left\{\mathbf{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{(p+q)\times(p+q)}:0\preceq\mathbf{X}\preceq I_{p+q},\ \operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{X})=r\right\}.

This problem is also solved using ADMM. Like in
Gao et al. (2017)
, it requires
computing an SVD at each iteration, but the matrix size is now
(p+q)\times(p+q)
.
The per-iteration SVD cost is therefore
O((p+q)^{3})
.

Stage 2: Thresholded Gradient Descent.

After initialization, refinement is performed via thresholded gradient descent.
The per-iteration complexity is

O\!\left(n(p+q)r+(p+q)\log(p+q)\right).

The first term corresponds to gradient computation, and the second to thresholding/sorting.

Overall, the bottleneck of SGCA is the repeated SVDs of
(p+q)\times(p+q)
matrices.

B.2
Algorithm for CCAR
3

The sparse and group-sparse versions of Step 2, and the profiled graph-sparse version
described below, can be written as

\widehat{\mathbf{\Theta}}=\operatorname*{argmin}_{\mathbf{\Theta}\in\mathcal{C}}\frac{1}{n}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{0}-\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}\mathbf{\Theta}\|_{F}^{2}+\rho\sum_{m=1}^{M}w_{m}\|\mathbf{\Theta}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}\cdot}\|_{F},

(16)

where
\mathcal{G}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{G}_{M}
form a partition of
[\tilde{p}]
,

w_{m}>0
are prespecified weights, and
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{\tilde{p}\times q}

is a closed linear subspace. For the sparse and group-sparse cases,

\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{R}^{\tilde{p}\times q}
. For the graph case,
\mathcal{C}
enforces
that the edge variable belongs to the column space of the incidence matrix.
The three special cases are as follows.

Sparse CCAR
3
.

Take

\tilde{p}=p,\quad\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}=\mathbf{X},\quad\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{0}=\mathbf{Y}_{0},\quad\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{R}^{p\times q},\quad\mathcal{G}_{m}=\{m\},\quad{w_{m}=1}.

Then
\widehat{\mathbf{B}}=\widehat{\mathbf{\Theta}}
.

Group-sparse CCAR
3
.

Take

\tilde{p}=p,\quad\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}=\mathbf{X},\quad\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{0}=\mathbf{Y}_{0},\quad\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{R}^{p\times q},\quad\mathcal{G}_{m}=g_{m},\quad w_{m}=\sqrt{|g_{m}|}.

Then
\widehat{\mathbf{B}}=\widehat{\mathbf{\Theta}}
.

Graph-sparse CCAR
3
.

Let
\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times p}
be the graph incidence matrix, where

m=|E|
, and let

\mathbf{\Pi}=\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\dagger}\mathbf{\Gamma}

be the orthogonal projector onto
\ker(\mathbf{\Gamma})
. Any coefficient matrix can be
written as

\mathbf{B}=\mathbf{B}_{\mathrm{null}}+\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\dagger}\mathbf{\Theta},

where
\mathbf{B}_{\mathrm{null}}\in\ker(\mathbf{\Gamma})
and

\mathbf{\Theta}=\mathbf{\Gamma}\mathbf{B}\in\operatorname{col}(\mathbf{\Gamma})^{\oplus q}
. Define the projection
onto the fitted null-space design by

\mathbf{P}_{\Pi}=\mathbf{X}\mathbf{\Pi}\bigl\{(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{\Pi})^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{\Pi})\bigr\}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{\Pi})^{\top}.

Profiling out the null-space component gives the exact profiled problem
(
16
) with

\tilde{p}=m,\qquad\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}=(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{P}_{\Pi})\mathbf{X}\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\dagger},\qquad\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{0}=(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{P}_{\Pi})\mathbf{Y}_{0},\qquad\mathcal{C}=\{\mathbf{\Theta}:(\mathbf{\Gamma}\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\dagger})\mathbf{\Theta}=\mathbf{\Theta}\},

and singleton edge groups
\mathcal{G}_{m}=\{m\}
with
w_{m}=1
. After obtaining

\widehat{\mathbf{\Theta}}
, the profiled null-space component is

\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{\mathrm{null}}=\mathbf{\Pi}\bigl\{(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{\Pi})^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{\Pi})\bigr\}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{\Pi})^{\top}\left(\mathbf{Y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\dagger}\widehat{\mathbf{\Theta}}\right),

and the final estimator is

\widehat{\mathbf{B}}=\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{\mathrm{null}}+\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\dagger}\widehat{\mathbf{\Theta}}.

The constraint
\widehat{\mathbf{\Theta}}\in\operatorname{col}(\mathbf{\Gamma})^{\oplus q}
is part of
the exact equivalence with the original penalty
\|\mathbf{\Gamma}\mathbf{B}\|_{21}
.

To solve (
16
), introduce a splitting variable

\mathbf{Z}\in\mathbb{R}^{\tilde{p}\times q}
and consider the constrained problem

\operatorname*{minimize}_{\mathbf{\Theta}\in\mathcal{C},\,\mathbf{Z}}\frac{1}{n}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{0}-\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}\mathbf{\Theta}\|_{F}^{2}+\rho\sum_{m=1}^{M}w_{m}\|\mathbf{Z}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}\cdot}\|_{F}\quad\text{subject to}\quad\mathbf{\Theta}=\mathbf{Z}.

Let
\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{C}}
denote the orthogonal projector onto
\mathcal{C}
.
Thus
\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{C}}=\mathbf{I}_{\tilde{p}}
for the sparse and group-sparse
cases, while
\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{C}}\mathbf{\Theta}=(\mathbf{\Gamma}\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\dagger})\mathbf{\Theta}
for
the graph-sparse case. Using the scaled augmented Lagrangian with scaled dual
variable
\mathbf{D}
,

\mathcal{L}_{\delta}(\mathbf{\Theta},\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{D})=\frac{1}{n}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{0}-\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}\mathbf{\Theta}\|_{F}^{2}+\rho\sum_{m=1}^{M}w_{m}\|\mathbf{Z}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}\cdot}\|_{F}+\frac{\delta}{2}\|\mathbf{\Theta}-\mathbf{Z}+\mathbf{D}\|_{F}^{2}-\frac{\delta}{2}\|\mathbf{D}\|_{F}^{2},

we obtain the ADMM updates given in Algorithm
3
.

Algorithm 3

ADMM for (
16
)

Input:

\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times\tilde{p}}
,

\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times q}
,
groups
\mathcal{G}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{G}_{M}
,
weights
w_{1},\ldots,w_{M}
,
regularization parameter
\rho>0
,
ADMM parameter
\delta>0
,
projector
\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{C}}
,
stopping tolerances
\epsilon_{\mathrm{pri}},\epsilon_{\mathrm{dual}}>0
.

Initialize:

\mathbf{\Theta}^{(0)},\mathbf{Z}^{(0)},\mathbf{D}^{(0)}\in\mathbb{R}^{\tilde{p}\times q}
.

Precompute:

\mathbf{K}_{\delta}=\frac{2}{n}\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}+\delta\mathbf{I}_{\tilde{p}}

and

\mathbf{C}=\frac{2}{n}\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{0}.

When
\tilde{p}
is moderate, factorize
\mathbf{K}_{\delta}
once and reuse the
factorization throughout; when
\tilde{p}\gg n
, the Woodbury identity described
below is more efficient.

Repeat until convergence:

1:

Update
\mathbf{\Theta}
by solving

\mathbf{K}_{\delta}\mathbf{\Theta}^{(t+1)}=\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{C}}\!\left\{\mathbf{C}+\delta\bigl(\mathbf{Z}^{(t)}-\mathbf{D}^{(t)}\bigr)\right\}.

2:

for

m=1,\ldots,M

do

3:

Update the block
\mathbf{Z}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}\cdot}
via group soft-thresholding

\mathbf{Z}^{(t+1)}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}\cdot}=\mathcal{S}_{\rho w_{m}/\delta}\!\left(\mathbf{\Theta}^{(t+1)}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}\cdot}+\mathbf{D}^{(t)}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}\cdot}\right),

where

\mathcal{S}_{\tau}(\mathbf{V})=\left(1-\frac{\tau}{\|\mathbf{V}\|_{F}}\right)_{+}\mathbf{V}

with the convention
\mathcal{S}_{\tau}(0)=0
.

4:

end

for

5:

Update the scaled dual variable:

\mathbf{D}^{(t+1)}=\mathbf{D}^{(t)}+\mathbf{\Theta}^{(t+1)}-\mathbf{Z}^{(t+1)}.

Output:

\widehat{\mathbf{\Theta}}
, and hence
\widehat{\mathbf{B}}
through the case-specific reconstruction above.

Define the primal and dual residuals by

\mathbf{r}^{(t+1)}=\mathbf{\Theta}^{(t+1)}-\mathbf{Z}^{(t+1)}

and

\mathbf{s}^{(t+1)}=\delta\bigl(\mathbf{Z}^{(t+1)}-\mathbf{Z}^{(t)}\bigr).

We terminate the iterations when

\|\mathbf{r}^{(t+1)}\|_{F}\leq\epsilon_{\mathrm{pri}}

and

\|\mathbf{s}^{(t+1)}\|_{F}\leq\epsilon_{\mathrm{dual}}.

Choice of
\delta
.

The ADMM parameter
\delta>0
does not affect the optimizer of
(
16
), but it influences convergence speed and numerical
stability. In the
\mathbf{\Theta}
-update, a larger
\delta
improves conditioning of

\mathbf{K}_{\delta}
, which is beneficial when
\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}

is ill-conditioned. In the
\mathbf{Z}
-update, a larger
\delta
gives a smaller
proximal threshold
\rho w_{m}/\delta
for that iteration, but this changes only the
ADMM trajectory, not the limiting optimizer. Overall,
\delta
controls the balance
between the primal and dual residuals. In all experiments, we use
\delta=1
, though
adaptive residual-balancing schemes can lead to faster convergence.

Computational cost.

For fixed
\delta
, the matrices
\mathbf{K}_{\delta}
and
\mathbf{C}
are constant
across iterations and may be precomputed once. With a dense factorization of

\mathbf{K}_{\delta}
, the one-time cost is

O(n\tilde{p}^{2}+\tilde{p}^{3}+n\tilde{p}q),

and each subsequent
\mathbf{\Theta}
-update costs
O(\tilde{p}^{2}q)
. The
\mathbf{Z}
- and

\mathbf{D}
-updates cost
O(\tilde{p}q)
, so the overall per-iteration cost is

O(\tilde{p}^{2}q)
after factorization.

When
\tilde{p}\gg n
, the Woodbury identity gives

\mathbf{K}_{\delta}^{-1}=\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbf{I}_{\tilde{p}}-\frac{2}{n\delta^{2}}\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{I}_{n}+\frac{2}{n\delta}\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}.

Thus it suffices to factorize an
n\times n
matrix. In that case the
precomputation cost is

O(n^{2}\tilde{p}+n^{3}+n\tilde{p}q)
,
and each
\mathbf{\Theta}
-update costs

O(n^{2}q+n\tilde{p}q)
.
This is preferable, for example, in graph-sparse CCAR
3
when
\tilde{p}=|E|\gg n
.

Appendix C
Proofs: ordinary CCAR
3

C.1
Proof of Lemma
1

Proof.

Using the change of variables
\mathbf{V}_{0}=\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{V}
and
\mathbf{Y}_{0}=\mathbf{Y}\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
, and the orthonormality of the columns of
\mathbf{V}_{0}
(i.e.
\mathbf{V}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{0}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
), we transform the objective in Equation (
5
) into

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{U}\|_{F}^{2}&=\|\mathbf{Y}_{0}\mathbf{V}_{0}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{U}\|_{F}^{2}\\
&=\underbrace{\mathop{\sf Tr}(\mathbf{V}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}_{0}\mathbf{V}_{0})}_{=rn}-2\mathop{\sf Tr}(\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}_{0}\mathbf{V}_{0})+\underbrace{\mathop{\sf Tr}(\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{U})}_{=rn}\\
&=\underbrace{\mathop{\sf Tr}(\mathbf{Y}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}_{0})}_{=qn}-2\mathop{\sf Tr}(\mathbf{V}_{0}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}_{0})+\underbrace{\mathop{\sf Tr}(\mathbf{V}_{0}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{V}_{0}^{\top})}_{=rn}+(r-q)n\\
&=\|\mathbf{Y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{V}_{0}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}+(r-q)n.\end{split}

The additive constant
(r-q)n
does not affect the minimizer, which proves the claim.
∎

C.2
Proof of Theorem
2

Proof.

By the Law of Large Numbers, since
p
and
q
are fixed,

\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}\xrightarrow{a.s.}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X},\qquad\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}\xrightarrow{a.s.}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y},\qquad\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{XY}\xrightarrow{a.s.}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{XY}.

Because
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}
and
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}
are positive definite, the sample
covariance matrices are positive definite for all sufficiently large
n
, almost surely.
Moreover, by continuity of the inverse and inverse-square-root maps on the positive
definite cone,

\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}^{-1}\xrightarrow{a.s.}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}^{-1},\qquad\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-1/2}\xrightarrow{a.s.}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}.

Therefore,

\widehat{\mathbf{B}}=\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}^{-1}\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{XY}\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-1/2}\xrightarrow{a.s.}\mathbf{B}^{\star}:=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}^{-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{XY}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}.

Under the canonical pair model,

\mathbf{B}^{\star}=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}^{-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{V}_{0}^{\top},\qquad\mathbf{V}_{0}:=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{1/2}\mathbf{V}.

Since

\mathbf{V}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{0}=\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}\mathbf{V}=\mathbf{I}_{r},

the columns of
\mathbf{V}_{0}
are orthonormal. We also note that
\mathbf{U}
has full column rank
because
\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\mathbf{U}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
. Hence

\operatorname{rank}(\mathbf{B}^{\star})=r.

Moreover,

\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{B}^{\star})=\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{U}\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{V}_{0}^{\top})\geq\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{U})\lambda_{r}>0.

Thus the
r
-dimensional left and right singular subspaces of
\mathbf{B}^{\star}
are
separated from the null singular subspace by a positive gap.

Let

\mathbf{B}^{\star}=\mathbf{U}_{B}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{B}\mathbf{V}_{B}^{\top}

be the rank-
r
singular value decomposition of
\mathbf{B}^{\star}
, where

\mathbf{U}_{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times r}
and

\mathbf{V}_{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{q\times r}
have orthonormal columns. Since

\mathbf{B}^{\star}=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{V}_{0}^{\top},

we have

\operatorname{span}(\mathbf{U}_{B})=\operatorname{span}(\mathbf{U}),\qquad\operatorname{span}(\mathbf{V}_{B})=\operatorname{span}(\mathbf{V}_{0}).

Because both
\mathbf{V}_{B}
and
\mathbf{V}_{0}
have orthonormal columns and span the same
subspace, there exists an orthogonal matrix
\mathbf{Q}_{Y}\in\mathcal{O}_{r}
such that

\mathbf{V}_{B}=\mathbf{V}_{0}\mathbf{Q}_{Y}.

Similarly, define

\mathbf{U}_{\diamond}:=\mathbf{U}_{B}\left(\mathbf{U}_{B}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\mathbf{U}_{B}\right)^{-1/2}.

Then

\mathbf{U}_{\diamond}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\mathbf{U}_{\diamond}=\mathbf{I}_{r},

and
\operatorname{span}(\mathbf{U}_{\diamond})=\operatorname{span}(\mathbf{U})
.
Since
\mathbf{U}
is also
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}
-orthonormal, there exists

\mathbf{Q}_{X}\in\mathcal{O}_{r}
such that

\mathbf{U}_{\diamond}=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{Q}_{X}.

To prove consistency, we apply a standard Davis–Kahan/Wedin singular-subspace perturbation bound to

\widehat{\mathbf{B}}
and
\mathbf{B}^{\star}
. Since

\widehat{\mathbf{B}}\to\mathbf{B}^{\star}
in Frobenius norm almost surely and

\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{B}^{\star})>0
, there exist sequences

\mathbf{O}_{n},\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{n}\in\mathcal{O}_{r}
such that

\|\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{B}}-\mathbf{U}_{B}\mathbf{O}_{n}\|_{F}\xrightarrow{a.s.}0,\qquad\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\hat{B}}-\mathbf{V}_{B}\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{n}\|_{F}\xrightarrow{a.s.}0.

We show that this implies the right singular-vector convergence to the estimator

\widehat{\mathbf{V}}
. Since
\mathbf{V}_{B}=\mathbf{V}_{0}\mathbf{Q}_{Y}
and

\mathbf{V}_{0}=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{1/2}\mathbf{V}
, we have

\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}\mathbf{V}_{B}=\mathbf{V}\mathbf{Q}_{Y}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}-\mathbf{V}\mathbf{Q}_{Y}\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{n}\|_{F}

\displaystyle=\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-1/2}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\hat{B}}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}\mathbf{V}_{B}\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{n}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-1/2}\|_{op}\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\hat{B}}-\mathbf{V}_{B}\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{n}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}\|_{op}\|\mathbf{V}_{B}\|_{F}\xrightarrow{a.s.}0.

Since
\mathbf{Q}_{Y}\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{n}\in\mathcal{O}_{r}
, this implies

\min_{\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathcal{O}_{r}}\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}-\mathbf{V}\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}\|_{F}\xrightarrow{a.s.}0.

We now turn to the proof of consistency for
\mathbf{U}
. Let

\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{n}:=\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{B}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{B}},\qquad\mathbf{A}:=\mathbf{U}_{B}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\mathbf{U}_{B}.

Since

\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{B}}-\mathbf{U}_{B}\mathbf{O}_{n}\xrightarrow{a.s.}0\quad\text{and}\quad\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}\xrightarrow{a.s.}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X},

we have

\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{n}-\mathbf{O}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{O}_{n}\xrightarrow{a.s.}0.

The matrix
\mathbf{A}
is positive definite. Hence, for all sufficiently large
n
,

\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{n}
is also positive definite almost surely, and by continuity of the
inverse-square-root map,

\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{n}^{-1/2}-\mathbf{O}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{-1/2}\mathbf{O}_{n}\xrightarrow{a.s.}0.

Equivalently,

\mathbf{O}_{n}\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{n}^{-1/2}-\mathbf{A}^{-1/2}\mathbf{O}_{n}\xrightarrow{a.s.}0.

Now

\widehat{\mathbf{U}}=\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{B}}\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{n}^{-1/2},\qquad\mathbf{U}_{\diamond}=\mathbf{U}_{B}\mathbf{A}^{-1/2}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mathbf{U}}-\mathbf{U}_{\diamond}\mathbf{O}_{n}\|_{F}

\displaystyle=\|\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{B}}\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{n}^{-1/2}-\mathbf{U}_{B}\mathbf{A}^{-1/2}\mathbf{O}_{n}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{B}}-\mathbf{U}_{B}\mathbf{O}_{n}\|_{F}\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{n}^{-1/2}\|_{op}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\mathbf{U}_{B}\ \|_{op}\|\mathbf{O}_{n}\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{n}^{-1/2}-\mathbf{A}^{-1/2}\mathbf{O}_{n}\|_{F}\xrightarrow{a.s.}0.

Since
\mathbf{U}_{\diamond}=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{Q}_{X}
, we obtain

\|\widehat{\mathbf{U}}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{Q}_{X}\mathbf{O}_{n}\|_{F}\xrightarrow{a.s.}0.

Because
\mathbf{Q}_{X}\mathbf{O}_{n}\in\mathcal{O}_{r}
, it follows that

\min_{\mathbf{O}\in\mathcal{O}_{r}}\|\widehat{\mathbf{U}}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{O}\|_{F}\xrightarrow{a.s.}0.

This completes the proof.
∎

Appendix D
Proofs: sparse CCAR
3

D.1
Useful lemmas

This subsection provides a list of the technical lemmas adapted from
Gao et al. (2017)
and
Gao et al. (2015)
that we used in this manuscript to derive our theoretical results.

Lemma 5

(Adaptation of Lemma 3 in
Gao et al. (2015)
)
.

Assume
\frac{s_{u}+q}{n}<c_{0}
for
c_{0}\in(0,1)
. Consider a deterministic set
T_{u}\in[p]
with
|T_{u}|=s_{u}
.
For any constant
C^{\prime}>0
and assuming that
c_{0}
is small enough,
there exists a constant
C>0
that depends solely on
C^{\prime}
and
c_{0}
, such that:

\begin{split}\left\|[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{T_{u}T_{u}}-[\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}]_{T_{u}T_{u}}\right\|_{op}^{2}\leq\frac{CM^{2}}{n}s_{u}\\
\left\|[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]^{\frac{1}{2}}_{T_{u}T_{u}}-[{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]^{\frac{1}{2}}_{T_{u}T_{u}}\right\|_{op}^{2}\leq\frac{CM^{2}}{n}s_{u}\\
\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}-{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}\right\|_{op}^{2}\leq\frac{CM^{2}}{n}q\\
\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{Y}-{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{Y}\right\|_{op}^{2}\leq\frac{CM^{2}}{n}q\\
\end{split}

(17)

with probability at least
1-\exp\{-C^{\prime}s_{u}\}-\exp\{-C^{\prime}q\}.

Proof.

We adapt Lemma 3 of
Gao et al. (2015)
to explicit the dependency on
M
. Note that the set
T_{u}
considered here is deterministic.
Therefore, by Lemma 6.5 of
Wainwright (2019)
, there are positive universal constants
\{c_{j}\}_{j=0}^{3}
such that, for any
\delta\in(0,1)
:

\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{\left\|[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{T_{u}T_{u}}-[\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}]_{T_{u}T_{u}}\right\|_{op}}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\right\|_{op}}>c_{1}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{s_{u}}{n}}+{\frac{s_{u}}{n}}\Big)+\delta\right]\leq c_{2}\exp\big\{-c_{3}n(\delta\wedge\delta^{2})\big\}.

Denote
c_{2}=e^{s_{u}\gamma_{2}}
and choose
\delta^{2}=\frac{s_{u}}{n}\frac{C^{\prime}+\gamma_{2}}{c_{3}}
. Assuming that
c_{0}
is small enough (i.e.
c_{0}\leq\frac{c_{3}}{C^{\prime}+\gamma_{2}}
) so that
\delta\leq 1
(and thus,
\delta^{2}\leq\delta
) we have:

\mathbb{P}\left[\left\|[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{T_{u}T_{u}}-[\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}]_{T_{u}T_{u}}\right\|_{op}>c_{1}^{\prime}M\sqrt{\frac{s_{u}}{n}}\right]\leq\exp\{-Cs_{u}\}

for a choice of
c_{1}^{\prime}=2c_{1}+\sqrt{\frac{C^{\prime}+\gamma_{2}}{c_{3}}}\leq 2c_{1}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{c_{0}}}
.
The same result holds for
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}
replacing
s_{u}
by
q
.
Finally, for the operator norm of the square roots, by Lemma 2 of the supplementary material of
Gao et al. (2015)
, we have:

\left\|[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]^{\frac{1}{2}}_{T_{u}T_{u}}-[{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]^{\frac{1}{2}}_{T_{u}T_{u}}\right\|_{op}\leq\frac{\left\|[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{T_{u}T_{u}}-[\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}]_{T_{u}T_{u}}\right\|_{op}}{\sigma_{\min}\Big([\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]^{\frac{1}{2}}_{T_{u}T_{u}}\Big)+\sigma_{\min}\Big([{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]^{\frac{1}{2}}_{T_{u}T_{u}}\Big)}

Let us assume for now that
\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\right\|_{op}=1,
and take
M=1.

Let
\mathcal{A}_{X}
denote the event
\mathcal{A}_{X}=\Big\{\left\|[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{T_{u}T_{u}}-[\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}]_{T_{u}T_{u}}\right\|_{op}\leq c_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{\frac{s_{u}}{n}}\Big\}
, with
c^{\prime}_{1}
as previously defined. Then, on
\mathcal{A}_{X}
, by Weyl’s inequality
(
Giraud, 2021
)
,

\sigma_{\min}\Big([\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]^{\frac{1}{2}}_{T_{u}T_{u}}\Big)=\sigma_{\min}\Big([\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{T_{u}T_{u}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\geq\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}\Big([{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{T_{u}T_{u}}\Big)-c_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{\frac{s_{u}}{n}}}.

Therefore, as long as
c_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{\frac{s_{u}}{n}}\leq\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X})
, which happens as soon as
c_{0}\leq\left(\frac{\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X})}{c^{\prime}_{1}}\right)^{2}
, we have:

\left\|[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]^{\frac{1}{2}}_{T_{u}T_{u}}-[{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]^{\frac{1}{2}}_{T_{u}T_{u}}\right\|_{op}\leq\frac{c_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{\frac{s_{u}}{n}}}{\frac{3}{2}\sigma_{\min}\Big([{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]^{\frac{1}{2}}_{T_{u}T_{u}}\Big)}.

Scaling the previous inequality by
\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{op}
, and using the fact that
\sigma_{\min}\Big(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{M}}
, we have:

\left\|[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]^{\frac{1}{2}}_{T_{u}T_{u}}-[{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]^{\frac{1}{2}}_{T_{u}T_{u}}\right\|_{op}\leq M{c_{1}^{\prime\prime}\sqrt{\frac{s_{u}}{n}}}.

The proof for
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{\frac{1}{2}}
is similar and considers instead the event

\mathcal{A}_{Y}=\Big\{\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}\right\|_{op}>c_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{\frac{q}{n}}\Big\}.

∎

The following lemma is a direct consequence of the previous lemma by applying a union bound.

Lemma 6

(Adapted from Lemma 12 in
Gao et al. (2015)
)
.

Let
S_{u}
be a set of indices of size
|S_{u}|=s_{u}
.
Assume
\frac{q+s_{u}\log\left(\frac{ep}{s_{u}}\right)}{n}<c_{0}
for some constant
c_{0}\in(0,1)
. For any constant
C^{\prime}>0
, there exists some constant
C>0
only depending on
C^{\prime}
and
c_{0}
such that:

\left\|[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{S_{u}S_{u}}-[{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{S_{u}S_{u}}\right\|_{op}^{2}\leq\frac{CM^{2}}{n}s_{u}\log\left(\frac{ep}{s_{u}}\right)

\left\|[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]^{\frac{1}{2}}_{S_{u}S_{u}}-[{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]^{\frac{1}{2}}_{S_{u}S_{u}}\right\|_{op}^{2}\leq\frac{CM^{2}}{n}s_{u}\log\left(\frac{ep}{s_{u}}\right)

\left\|{\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}}-{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}\right\|_{op}^{2}\leq\frac{CM^{2}}{n}q

\left\|{\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}}^{\frac{1}{2}}-{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{Y}\right\|_{op}^{2}\leq\frac{CM^{2}}{n}q

with probability at least
1-\exp\{-C^{\prime}q\}-\exp\big\{-C^{\prime}s_{u}\log\big(\frac{ep}{s_{u}}\big)\big\}
.

Proof.

Bounding over all possible sets
T_{u}\subset[p]
with cardinality at most
s_{u}
, we obtain:

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left[\left\|[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{S_{u}S_{u}}-[{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{S_{u}S_{u}}\right\|_{op}\geq t\right]&\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{T_{u}\in[p]:\penalty\ |T_{u}|=s_{u}}\left\|[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{T_{u}T_{u}}-[{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{T_{u}T_{u}}\right\|_{op}\geq t\right]\\
&\leq\sum_{T_{u}\in[p]}\mathbb{P}\left[\left\|[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{T_{u}T_{u}}-[{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{T_{u}T_{u}}\right\|_{op}\geq t\right]\\
&\leq{p\choose{s_{u}}}\mathbb{P}\left[\left\|[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{T_{u}T_{u}}-[{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{T_{u}T_{u}}\right\|_{op}\geq t\right]\end{split}

(18)

Therefore, noting that
{p\choose{s_{u}}}\leq\left(\frac{ep}{s_{u}}\right)^{s_{u}}
, for any
\delta>0
, by the proof of Lemma
5
:

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left[\left\|[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{S_{u}S_{u}}-[{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{S_{u}S_{u}}\right\|_{op}\geq\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\right\|_{op}\Bigg(c_{1}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{s_{u}}{n}}+{\frac{s_{u}}{n}}\Big)+\delta\Bigg)\right]\\
\leq c_{2}\exp\Big\{s_{u}\log\left(\frac{ep}{s_{u}}\right)-c_{3}n(\delta\wedge\delta^{2})\Big\}\end{split}

(19)

Writing
c_{2}=e^{\gamma_{2}s_{u}\log\left(\frac{ep}{s_{u}}\right)}
for some constant
\gamma_{2}
, we choose
\delta^{2}=\frac{C^{\prime}+1+(\gamma_{2})_{+}}{c_{3}}\cdot\frac{s_{u}\log\left(\frac{ep}{s_{u}}\right)}{n}
for a constant
C^{\prime}>0
.
Assuming that
c_{0}
is small enough so that
\delta\leq 1
, which happens as soon as
c_{0}\leq\frac{c_{3}}{C^{\prime}+1+(\gamma_{2})_{+}}
, we have:

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\left\|[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{S_{u}S_{u}}-[{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{S_{u}S_{u}}\right\|_{op}\geq\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\right\|_{op}\Bigg(c_{1}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{s_{u}}{n}}+{\frac{s_{u}}{n}}\Big)+\sqrt{\frac{c_{3}+1+(\gamma_{2})_{+}}{c_{3}}\cdot\frac{s_{u}\log\big(\frac{ep}{s_{u}}\big)}{n}}\Bigg)\Bigg]\\
&\leq\exp\Big\{-\big(C^{\prime}+1+(\gamma_{2})_{+}-1-\gamma_{2}\big)\penalty\ s_{u}\log\left(\frac{ep}{s_{u}}\right)\Big\}\end{split}

implying that

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\left\|[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{S_{u}S_{u}}-[{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{S_{u}S_{u}}\right\|_{op}\geq c_{1}^{\prime}\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\right\|_{op}\sqrt{\frac{s_{u}\log\big(\frac{ep}{s_{u}}\big)}{n}}\Bigg]&\leq\exp\Big\{-C^{\prime}s_{u}\log\left(\frac{ep}{s_{u}}\right)\Big\}\\
\end{split}

for
c_{1}^{\prime}=2c_{1}+\sqrt{\frac{C^{\prime}+1+(\gamma_{2})_{+}}{c_{3}}}\leq 2c_{1}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{c_{0}}}.

The proof for the square root matrices is identical to the proof of the previous lemma (Lemma
5
), and also requires that
c_{0}
is small enough in the sense that
c_{0}\leq\left(\frac{\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X})}{c^{\prime}_{1}}\right)^{2}
.
∎

Lemma 7

(Adapted from Lemma 6.7 of
Gao et al. (2017)
)
.

Assume
\frac{{q}+\log p}{n}\leq c_{0}
for some sufficiently small constant
c_{0}\in(0,1)
. For
C^{\prime}>0
, there exists a constant
C>0
only depending on
C^{\prime}
and
c_{0}
such that

\max_{j\in[p]}\Big\|\big[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{XY}\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}-\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}\mathbf{B}^{*}\big]_{j\cdot}\Big\|_{2}\leq CM^{2}\sqrt{\frac{q+\log p}{n}},

with probability at least

{1-\exp\{-C^{\prime}q\}-\exp\{-C^{\prime}s_{u}\log(ep/s_{u})\}-\exp\{-C^{\prime}(q+\log p)\}}-\exp\{-C^{\prime}(r+\log p)\}.

Proof.

Recall that we defined
\mathbf{B}^{*}
as

\mathbf{B}^{*}=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{\frac{1}{2}}
. By definition of
\mathbf{B}^{*}
, we know that
{\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\mathbf{B}^{*}=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{XY}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}
. We thus have

\begin{split}\max_{j\in[p]}\Big\|\big[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{XY}\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}&-\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}\mathbf{B}^{*}\big]_{j\cdot}\Big\|\leq\underbrace{\max_{j\in[p]}\Big\|\big[(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{XY}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{XY})\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big]_{j\cdot}\Big\|}_{(A)}\\
&+\underbrace{\max_{j\in[p]}\Big\|\big[({\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{XY}(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}-{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}})\big]_{j\cdot}\Big\|}_{(B)}+\underbrace{\max_{j\in[p]}\Big\|\big[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}\mathbf{B}^{*}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\mathbf{B}^{*}\big]_{j\cdot}\Big\|}_{(C)}\end{split}

(20)

For a given constant
C^{\prime}>0
, let
\mathcal{A}
be the event:

\begin{split}\mathcal{A}=\Big\{\left\|[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]^{\frac{1}{2}}_{S_{u}S_{u}}-[\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}]^{\frac{1}{2}}_{S_{u}S_{u}}\right\|_{op}^{2}&\leq\frac{CM^{2}}{n}s_{u}\\
\left\|{\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}}^{\frac{1}{2}}-{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{Y}\right\|_{op}^{2}&\leq\frac{CM^{2}}{n}q\Big\}\\
\end{split}

(21)

where
S_{u}=\text{supp}(\mathbf{U}),
and where the constant
C
was chosen appropriately such that, by Lemma
5
, we know that
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{A}]\geq 1-\exp\{-C^{\prime}q\}-\exp\{-C^{\prime}s_{u}\}
.

Bound for (A).

We note that

\begin{split}\max_{j\in[p]}\Big\|\big[(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{XY}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{XY})\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big]_{j\cdot}\Big\|&=\max_{j\in[p]}\Big\|\big[(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{XY}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{XY})\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big]_{j\cdot}\Big\|\\
&\leq\max_{j\in[p]}\Big\|\big[(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{XY}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{XY})\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big]_{j\cdot}\Big\|\penalty\ \left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{op}\\
\end{split}

By Weyl’s theorem
(
Giraud, 2021
)
, we know that on the event
\mathcal{A}
:

\forall i\in[q],\quad\big|\sigma_{i}\big(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{Y}\big)-\sigma_{i}\big(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{Y}\big)\big|\leq\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{Y}-\mathbf{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{Y}\right\|_{op}\leq\sqrt{CM^{2}\frac{q}{n}}.

Therefore, writing
\delta_{q,n}=\sqrt{\frac{q}{n}}
and assuming
\sqrt{C}M\delta_{q,n}\leq\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{\min}\big(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)
, we have:

\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{Y}\right\|_{op}=\frac{1}{\sigma_{\min}\big(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{Y}\big)}\leq\frac{1}{\sigma_{\min}\big(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{Y}\big)-\sqrt{C}M\delta_{q,n}}\leq\frac{2}{\sigma_{\min}\big(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{Y}\big)}.

(22)

Let
\mathbf{Z}_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}_{p}(0,\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X})
,
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}_{q}(0,\mathbf{I}_{q})
, and
\mathbf{1}_{j}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{1\times p}
the vector indicator of row
j
(i.e.
[\mathbf{1}_{j}]_{i}=0
for all
i\neq j
, and
[\mathbf{1}_{j}]_{j}=1
). Thus, we may write

\mathbf{1}_{j}^{\top}\big(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{XY}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{XY}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\mathbf{1}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{i}\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{1}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{i}\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}^{\top})\big).

Define for each
j\in[p]
the matrix

\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{1}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{i}\\
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}\end{pmatrix}.

Since
\mathbf{1}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{i}\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}^{\top}
is a submatrix of
\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}(\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)})^{\top}
, we have:

\begin{split}\max_{j\in[p]}\Big\|\mathbf{1}_{j}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{XY}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{XY}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}})\Big\|&\leq\max_{j\in[p]}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}\big(\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}\big)^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}\big(\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}\big)^{\top}\big)\right\|_{op}\\
\end{split}

(23)

Denoting
\mathbf{\mathcal{H}}^{(j)}=\mathbb{E}\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}\big(\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}\big)^{\top}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{1}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\mathbf{1}_{j}&\mathbf{1}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{XY}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\\
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{YX}\mathbf{1}_{j}&\mathbf{I}_{q}\end{pmatrix}
, this implies:

\begin{split}\left\|\mathbf{\mathcal{H}}^{(j)}\right\|_{op}&=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}u_{1}\in\mathbb{R},\penalty\ u_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{q}\\
u_{1}^{2}+\|u_{2}\|^{2}=1\end{subarray}}\mathbf{1}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\mathbf{1}_{j}u_{1}^{2}+2u_{1}\mathbf{1}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{XY}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}u_{2}+{u_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{I}_{q}u_{2}}\\
&\leq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}u_{1}\in\mathbb{R},\penalty\ u_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{q}\\
u_{1}^{2}+\|u_{2}\|^{2}=1\end{subarray}}Mu_{1}^{2}+2\lambda_{1}\sqrt{M}_{1}u_{1}\|u_{2}\|+\|u_{2}\|^{2}\\
&\leq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}u_{1}\in\mathbb{R},\penalty\ u_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{q}\\
u_{1}^{2}+\|u_{2}\|^{2}=1\end{subarray}}Mu_{1}^{2}+\lambda_{1}\sqrt{M}(u_{1}^{2}+\|u_{2}\|^{2})+\|u_{2}\|^{2}\\
&=M(1+\lambda_{1})\leq 2M,\\
\end{split}

(24)

since by assumption
\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\right\|_{op}\leq M
and
\lambda_{1}\leq 1.

By Theorem 6.5 of
Wainwright (2019)
, there exists universal positive constants
\{c_{j}\}_{j=1}^{3}
such that, for any
t>0
:

\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}\big(\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}\big)^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}\big(\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}\big)^{\top}\big)\right\|_{op}\geq 4Mc_{1}\sqrt{\frac{1+q}{n}}+2Mt\Bigg]\leq c_{2}\exp\big\{-c_{3}n(t\wedge t^{2})\big\}.

Thus, by a simple union bound, we conclude that:

\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\max_{j\in[p]}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}\big(\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}\big)^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}\big(\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}\big)^{\top}\big)\right\|_{op}\geq 4Mc_{1}\sqrt{\frac{1+q}{n}}+2Mt\Bigg]\leq pc_{2}\exp\big\{-c_{3}n(t\wedge t^{2})\big\}.

We write
c_{2}=e^{\gamma_{2}(q+\log p)}
, for
\gamma_{2}
a constant.
Let
C^{\prime}>0
be a constant and taking
{t^{2}=\frac{C^{\prime}+1+(\gamma_{2})_{+}}{c_{3}}\cdot\frac{q+\log p}{n}}
, and assuming that
c_{0}
is small enough (i.e.
c_{0}\leq\frac{c_{3}}{C^{\prime}+1+(\gamma_{2})_{+}}
) so that
t\leq 1
, the previous equation implies

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\max_{j\in[p]}&\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}\big(\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}\big)^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}\big(\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}\big)^{\top}\big)\right\|_{op}\\
&\geq 4Mc_{1}\sqrt{\frac{1+q}{n}}+2M\sqrt{\frac{C^{\prime}+1+(\gamma_{2})_{+}}{c_{3}}\cdot\frac{q+\log p}{n}}\Bigg]\\
&\leq\exp\big\{\gamma_{2}(q+\log p)+\big(1-C^{\prime}-1-(\gamma_{2})_{+}\big)\log p-\big(C^{\prime}+1+(\gamma_{2})_{+}\big)q\big\}\\
&=\exp\big\{-C^{\prime}\log p-(C^{\prime}+1)q\big\}\\
&\leq\exp\big\{-C^{\prime}(\log p+q))\big\}.\\
\end{split}

(25)

Therefore, we can choose
c_{1}^{\prime}=4c_{1}+2\sqrt{\frac{C^{\prime}+1+(\gamma_{2})_{+}}{c_{3}}}
such that, as soon as
p\geq 3
:

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\max_{j\in[p]}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}\big(\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}\big)^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}\big(\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}\big)^{\top})\right\|_{op}\geq c_{1}^{\prime}M\sqrt{\frac{q+\log p}{n}}\Bigg]\\
&\leq\exp\big\{-C^{\prime}(q+\log p)\big\}\end{split}

(26)

Let us denote as
\mathcal{A}_{1}
the event:

\mathcal{A}_{1}=\Bigg\{\max_{j\in[p]}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}\big(\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}\big)^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}\big(\mathbf{H}_{i}^{(j)}\big)^{\top})\right\|_{op}\geq c_{1}^{\prime}M\sqrt{\frac{q+\log p}{n}}\Bigg\}.

Therefore, with probability at least
1-\exp\{-C^{\prime}(q+\log p)\}-\exp\{-C^{\prime}q\}-\exp\{-C^{\prime}s_{u}\},
the event
\mathcal{A}\cap\mathcal{A}_{1}^{c}
occurs and

\begin{split}\max_{j\in[p]}\Big\|\big[(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{XY}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{XY})\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big]_{j\cdot}\Big\|\leq Mc_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{\frac{q+\log p}{n}}\cdot\frac{\sigma_{\max}\big(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{Y}\big)}{\sigma_{\min}\big(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{Y}\big)}\leq M^{2}c_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{\frac{q+\log p}{n}}\end{split}

(27)

Bound (B).
We have, on the event
\mathcal{A}
(and provided that
c_{0}
is small enough, in the sense of the proof of Lemma
7
):

\begin{split}\max_{j\in[p]}\Big\|\big[({\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{XY}(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}-{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}})\big]_{j\cdot}\Big\|&\leq\max_{j\in[p]}\Big\|\big[{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{XY}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big]_{j\cdot}\Big\|\penalty\ \left\|{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}-\mathbf{I}_{q}\right\|_{op}\\
&\leq\left\|{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{XY}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{op}\penalty\ \left\|{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{\frac{1}{2}}-\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{op}\penalty\ \left\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{op}.\\
&\leq\sqrt{M}\lambda_{1}\cdot\sqrt{C}M\sqrt{\frac{q}{n}}\cdot\frac{2}{\sigma_{\min}\big(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{Y}\big)}\\
&=2\sqrt{C}\sqrt{\frac{q}{n}}M^{\frac{3}{2}}\cdot\frac{\lambda_{1}}{\sigma_{\min}\big(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{Y}\big)}\end{split}

(28)

Here we have used Inequality (
22
) to bound
\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{op}
on
\mathcal{A}
.
We thus obtain:

\begin{split}\max_{j\in[p]}\Big\|\big[({\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{XY}(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}-{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}})\big]_{j\cdot}\Big\|&\leq 2M^{2}\sqrt{C}\lambda_{1}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{q}{n}}\end{split}

(29)

Bound (C).
By the proof of Lemma 6.7 in
Gao et al. (2017)
:

\begin{split}\max_{j\in[p]}\Big\|\big[(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X})\mathbf{B}^{*}\big]_{j\cdot}\Big\|&=\max_{j\in[p]}\Big\|\big[(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X})\mathbf{U}\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big]_{j\cdot}\Big\|\\
&\leq\max_{j\in[p]}\frac{\Big\|\big[(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X})\mathbf{U}\big]_{j\cdot}\Big\|}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\right\|_{op}}\cdot\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\right\|_{op}\penalty\ \left\|\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{op}\\
&\leq C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{r+\log p}{n}}M\lambda_{1}\\
\end{split}

with probability at least
1-\exp\{-C^{\prime}(r+\log p)\},
for
C^{\prime}
a constant that can be chosen to be arbitrarily large, and
C_{2}
a constant that depends solely on
C^{\prime}
.
Let

\mathcal{A}_{2}=\Bigg\{\max_{j\in[p]}\Big\|\big[(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X})\mathbf{B}^{*}\big]_{j\cdot}\Big\|\geq C_{2}\lambda_{1}M\sqrt{\frac{r+\log p}{n}}\Bigg\}.

Combined bound (A) + (B) + (C).

Therefore, since
r<q
, and
\lambda<1
, on the event
\mathcal{A}\cap\mathcal{A}^{c}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}_{2}^{c}
, Inequality (
20
) becomes:

\begin{split}\max_{j\in[p]}\Big\|\big[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{XY}\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}-\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}\mathbf{B}^{*}\big]_{j\cdot}\Big\|&\leq c_{1}^{\prime}M^{2}\sqrt{\frac{q+\log p}{n}}+2\sqrt{C}M^{2}\sqrt{\frac{q}{n}}+{C}_{5}M\sqrt{\frac{r+\log p}{n}}\\
&\leq\widetilde{C}M^{2}\sqrt{\frac{q+\log p}{n}}\\
\end{split}

(30)

for an appropriate choice of
\widetilde{C}=3(c_{1}^{\prime}\vee 2\sqrt{C}\vee C_{2})
.
This occurs
with probability at least
{1-\exp\{-C^{\prime}q\}-\exp\{-C^{\prime}s_{u}\log(ep/s_{u})\}-\exp\{-C^{\prime}(q+\log p)\}}-\exp\{-C^{\prime}(r+\log p)\}.

∎

D.2
Proof of Theorem
3

Proof.

The proof of Theorem
3
is based on the same regression argument as before; the only population object that must
be used throughout is

\mathbf{B}^{*}=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{1/2}=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{V}_{0}^{\top},

because
\mathbf{Y}_{0}=\mathbf{Y}\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-1/2}
. In particular, under our model assumptions:

\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\mathbf{B}^{*}=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{1/2}=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{XY}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}.

Let
S_{u}={\rm{supp}}(\mathbf{B}^{*})={\rm{supp}}(\mathbf{U})
and define the high-probability score event

\mathcal{G}=\left\{\max_{j\in[p]}\left\|\left[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{XY}\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}\mathbf{B}^{*}\right]_{j\cdot}\right\|_{2}\leq CM^{2}\sqrt{\frac{q+\log p}{n}}\right\}.

let
\mathcal{A}
be the event:

\begin{split}\mathcal{A}=\Big\{{\left\|[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{S_{u}S_{u}}-[\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}]_{S_{u}S_{u}}\right\|_{op}^{2}}&\leq\frac{CM^{2}}{n}s_{u}\\
\left\|{\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}}-{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}\right\|_{op}^{2}&\leq\frac{CM^{2}}{n}q\\
\left\|{\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}}^{\frac{1}{2}}-{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{Y}\right\|_{op}^{2}&\leq\frac{CM^{2}}{n}q\Big\}\\
\end{split}

(31)

By Lemma
5
and the uniform sparse-spectrum event
\mathcal{E}_{\rm sp}
of Lemma
6
used below, since the set
S_{u}
is deterministic, we may work on an event
\mathcal{A}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\rm sp}
satisfying

\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{A}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\rm sp}]\geq 1-\exp\{-C^{\prime}q\}-\exp\{-C^{\prime}s_{u}\log(ep/s_{u})\}.

Bound on
{\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{\Delta}\right\|_{F}}
and
\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{F}
.

By the Basic Inequality:

\frac{1}{n}\Big\|\mathbf{Y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}\widehat{\mathbf{B}}\Big\|_{F}^{2}+\rho\|\widehat{\mathbf{B}}\Big\|_{21}\leq\frac{1}{n}\Big\|\mathbf{Y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}{\mathbf{B}}^{*}\|_{F}^{2}+\rho\|{\mathbf{B}}^{*}\|_{21}

Therefore, writing
\mathbf{\Delta}=\widehat{\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{B}^{*}
:

\begin{split}\frac{1}{n}\big\|\mathbf{X}\mathbf{\Delta}\big\|_{F}^{2}&\leq\frac{2}{n}\langle\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{B}^{*},\mathbf{\Delta}\rangle+\rho(\|\mathbf{B}^{*}\|_{21}-\|\mathbf{\Delta}+\mathbf{B}^{*}\|_{21})\\
\left\|{\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\mathbf{\Delta}\right\|_{F}^{2}&\leq 2\langle\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{XY}\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}-\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}{\mathbf{B}}^{*},\mathbf{\Delta}\rangle+\rho(\|\mathbf{B}^{*}_{S_{u}\cdot}\|_{21}-\|\mathbf{B}^{*}_{S_{u}\cdot}+\mathbf{\Delta}_{S_{u}\cdot}\|_{21}-\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{S_{u}^{c}\cdot}\|_{21})\\
&\leq 2\max_{j\in[p]}\Big\|\big[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{XY}{\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}-\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}{\mathbf{B}}^{*}\big]_{j\cdot}\Big\|\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{p}\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{j\cdot}\|+\rho(\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{S_{u}\cdot}\|_{21}-\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{S_{u}^{c}\cdot}\|_{21})\end{split}

where the last line follows by an application of Hölder’s inequality to tackle the inner product term, and the reverse triangle inequality for the
\ell_{21}
terms.
If we select
\rho
such that

\rho>4\max_{j\in[p]}\Big\|\big[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{XY}\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}-\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}{\mathbf{B}}^{*}\big]_{j\cdot}\Big\|

(32)

(and we will provide a value of
\rho
for which this holds in a later paragraph), then

\begin{split}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{\Delta}\right\|_{F}^{2}&\leq\frac{3\rho}{2}\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{S_{u}\cdot}\|_{21}-\frac{\rho}{2}\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{S_{u}^{c}\cdot}\|_{21}\\
\end{split}

(33)

Since
\sum_{j\in S_{u}}\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{j\cdot}\|\leq\Big(s_{u}\sum_{j\in S_{u}}\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{j\cdot}\|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}
, the upper bound becomes

\begin{split}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{\Delta}\right\|_{F}^{2}&\leq\frac{3\rho\sqrt{s_{u}}}{2}\Bigg(\sum_{j\in S_{u}}\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{j\cdot}\|^{2}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\end{split}

(34)

Note that Equation (
34
) also provides a cone inequality:

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in S_{u}^{c}}\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{j\cdot}\|\leq 3\sum_{j\in S_{u}}\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{j\cdot}\|

(35)

For an integer
t
, let the set
J_{1}=\{j_{1},\cdots,j_{t}\}
in
S_{u}^{c}
correspond to the
t
rows with the largest
\ell_{2}
norm in
\mathbf{\Delta}
, and define the extended support
\widetilde{S}_{u}=S_{u}\cup J_{1}
.
To proceed, we adopt the peeling strategy of
Gao et al. (2017)
. We partition
\widetilde{S}_{u}^{c}
into disjoint subsets
J_{2},...,J_{K}
of size
t
(with
|J_{K}|\leq t
), such that
J_{k}
is the set of (row) indices corresponding to the
t
largest row
\ell_{2}
norms in
\mathbf{\Delta}
outside
\widetilde{S}_{u}\bigcup_{j=2}^{k-1}J_{j}
. By the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{\Delta}\right\|_{F}\geq\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\widetilde{S}_{u}\cdot}\right\|_{F}-{\sum_{k=2}^{K}}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{\Delta}_{J_{k}\cdot}\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{{\sigma_{\min}^{\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}}(s_{u}+t)}}\cdot\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{\widetilde{S}_{u}\cdot}\right\|_{F}-\sqrt{{\sigma_{\max}^{\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}}(t)}}\cdot{\sum_{k=2}^{K}}\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{J_{k}\cdot}\right\|_{F}.

where
\sigma_{\min}^{\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}}(t+s_{u})
indicates the minimum eigenvalue of
\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}
on any set of size at most
s_{u}+t
, while
\sigma_{\max}^{\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}}(t)
denotes the maximum eigenvalue of
\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}
on any set of size at most
t
.
By construction, we also have:

\displaystyle\sum_{k=2}^{K}\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{J_{k}\cdot}\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\sum_{k=2}^{K}\max_{j\in J_{k}}\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{j\cdot}\|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}\sum_{k=2}^{K}\sum_{j\in J_{k-1}}\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{j\cdot}\|

(36)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}\sum_{j\in S_{u}^{c}}\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{j\cdot}\|\leq\frac{3}{\sqrt{t}}\sum_{j\in S_{u}}\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{j\cdot}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 3\sqrt{\frac{s_{u}}{t}}\Bigg(\sum_{j\in S_{u}}\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{j\cdot}\|^{2}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq 3\sqrt{\frac{s_{u}}{t}}\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{\widetilde{S}_{u}\cdot}\right\|_{F}.

(37)

In the above derivation, we have used the construction of
J_{k}
and the cone condition (
35
). Hence,

\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{\Delta}\right\|_{F}\geq\kappa\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{\widetilde{S}_{u}\cdot}\right\|_{F}

with
\kappa=\sqrt{{\sigma_{\min}^{\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}}(s_{u}+t)}}-3\sqrt{\frac{s_{u}}{t}\sigma_{\max}^{\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}}(t)}
.

In view of Lemma
6
(on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\rm sp}
) and by direct application of Weyl’s inequality
(
Giraud, 2021
)
, taking
t=c_{1}s_{u}
for some sufficiently large constant
c_{1}
, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\rm sp}
,
\kappa
can be lower-bounded by a positive constant
\kappa_{0}
only depending on
\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X})
,
c_{0}
and
c_{1}
:

\begin{split}\kappa&\geq\sqrt{\frac{1}{M}}-M\sqrt{{C\frac{(c_{1}+1)s_{u}\log\big(\frac{ep}{(c_{1}+1)s_{u}}\big)}{n}}}-3\sqrt{\frac{1}{c_{1}}}\cdot\Bigg(\sqrt{M}+M\sqrt{C\frac{c_{1}s_{u}\log\big(\frac{ep}{c_{1}s_{u}}\big)}{n}}\Bigg)\\
&\geq\sqrt{\frac{1}{M}}-3\sqrt{\frac{M}{c_{1}}}-M\left(\sqrt{{C\frac{(c_{1}+1)s_{u}\log\big(\frac{ep}{s_{u}}\big)}{n}}}+3\sqrt{C\frac{s_{u}\log\big(\frac{ep}{s_{u}}\big)}{n}}\right)\\
&\geq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{M}}-M\left(\sqrt{{C\frac{(c_{1}+1)s_{u}\log\big(\frac{ep}{s_{u}}\big)}{n}}}+3\sqrt{C\frac{s_{u}\log\big(\frac{ep}{s_{u}}\big)}{n}}\right)\\
&{\quad\text{choosing $c_{1}$ sufficiently large depending only on $M$}}\\
&\geq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{M}}{-M\sqrt{Cc_{0}}\left(\sqrt{c_{1}+1}+3\right)}\\
&{\geq\frac{1}{4\sqrt{M}}\qquad\text{for $c_{0}$ sufficiently small, depending only on $M$ and $c_{1}$.}}\\
\end{split}

Now, assuming that
c_{1}
is chosen as above and
c_{0}
is small enough,

\kappa\geq\gamma_{0}\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X})}\geq\gamma_{0}\frac{1}{\sqrt{M}}

where
\gamma_{0}\geq\frac{1}{4}
is a constant.
Combined with (
34
), this lower bound means that we have:

\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{\widetilde{S}_{u}\cdot}\right\|_{F}\leq{C\frac{\rho\sqrt{s_{u}}}{2\kappa_{0}^{2}}}\leq{C\frac{\rho\sqrt{s_{u}}}{2\gamma_{0}^{2}\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X})}}.

(38)

By (
36
)-(
37
), we have

\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{\widetilde{S}_{u}^{c}\cdot}\right\|_{F}\leq{\sum_{k=2}^{K}}\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{J_{k}\cdot}\right\|_{F}\leq 3\sqrt{\frac{s_{u}}{t}}\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{\widetilde{S}_{u}\cdot}\right\|_{F}\leq\frac{3}{\sqrt{c_{1}}}\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{\widetilde{S}_{u}\cdot}\right\|_{F}.

(39)

Summing (
38
) and (
39
), we have
\|{\mathbf{\Delta}}\|_{F}\leq C_{0}\frac{\sqrt{s_{u}}\rho}{\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X})}
,
therefore, Equation (
34
) becomes

\begin{split}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{\Delta}\right\|_{F}^{2}&\leq C_{0}\frac{3\rho^{2}s_{u}}{2\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X})}\leq C_{0}\frac{3M\rho^{2}s_{u}}{2}\end{split}

(40)

where
C_{0}
is a generic constant that depends on
\gamma_{0}
.

By Lemma
7
, we may choose
\rho\geq M^{2}C_{u}\sqrt{\frac{q+\log p}{n}}
for some large
C_{u}
so that (
32
) holds on the score event
\mathcal{G}
defined above. We work on
\mathcal{A}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\rm sp}\cap\mathcal{G}
; by a union bound, this event has at least the probability stated in the theorem.
In this case:

\begin{split}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{\Delta}\right\|_{F}^{2}&\leq C_{0}M^{5}\frac{3C_{u}^{2}s_{u}}{2}\cdot\frac{q+\log p}{n}\leq C^{\prime}M^{5}s_{u}\cdot\frac{q+\log p}{n}\\
\|{\mathbf{\Delta}}\|_{F}&\leq C^{\prime\prime}M^{3}\sqrt{\frac{s_{u}(q+\log p)}{n}}\end{split}

(41)

with high probability, with
C^{\prime}=\frac{3}{2}C_{0}C_{u}^{2}
, and
C^{\prime\prime}=C_{0}C_{u}
. The second line implies
\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{F}^{2}\leq(C^{\prime\prime})^{2}M^{6}s_{u}(q+\log p)/n
. This concludes the proof of Equation
10
.

Bound on
|{\rm{supp}}{\widehat{\mathbf{B}}}|
.

We note that, by the KKT conditions for all
j
, it holds

\frac{2}{n}\big[\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\widehat{\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{Y}_{0})\big]_{j\cdot}{\in-\rho\,\partial\|\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{j\cdot}\|_{2}}

This implies that:

\begin{cases}\frac{1}{n}\Big\|\big[\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\widehat{\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{Y}_{0})\big]_{j\cdot}\Big\|=\frac{\rho}{2}\qquad\text{ if }\qquad\big\|\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{j\cdot}\big\|\neq 0\\
\frac{1}{n}\Big\|\big[\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\widehat{\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{Y}_{0})\big]_{j\cdot}\Big\|\leq\frac{\rho}{2}\qquad\text{ if }\qquad\big\|\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{j\cdot}\big\|=0\\
\end{cases}

(42)

Let
T={\rm{supp}}{\widehat{\mathbf{B}}}
. The following inequality therefore holds for any
j\in T
on
\mathcal{G}
:

\frac{1}{n}\Big\|\big[\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{\Delta})\big]_{j\cdot}\Big\|\geq\frac{1}{n}\Big\|\big[\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\widehat{\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{Y}_{0})\big]_{j\cdot}\Big\|-\frac{1}{n}\Big\|\big[\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\mathbf{Y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{B}^{*})\big]_{j\cdot}\Big\|\geq\frac{\rho}{4}

(43)

Thus

\sum_{j\in{\rm{supp}}{\widehat{\mathbf{B}}}}\frac{1}{n^{2}}\Big\|\big[\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{\Delta})\big]_{j\cdot}\Big\|^{2}\geq\frac{\rho^{2}}{16}|T|.

Let

\widehat{M}=\max_{J\subset[p]:\penalty\ |J|=|T|}\left\|[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{JJ}\right\|_{op}.

Let
\mathbf{X}_{T}
denote the submatrix of
\mathbf{X}
with columns indexed by
T
. The previous inequality implies

\begin{split}|T|&\leq{\frac{16}{\rho^{2}}\frac{1}{n^{2}}\Big\|\mathbf{X}_{T}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{\Delta}\Big\|_{F}^{2}\leq\frac{16}{\rho^{2}}\frac{\left\|\mathbf{X}_{T}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{T}\right\|_{op}}{n^{2}}\|\mathbf{X}\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{F}^{2}}\\
&{\leq\frac{16\widehat{M}}{\rho^{2}}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{\Delta}\right\|_{F}^{2}\leq\frac{16\widehat{M}}{\rho^{2}}C_{0}\frac{3M\rho^{2}s_{u}}{2}\leq C_{1}\widehat{M}Ms_{u}}\\
\end{split}

(44)

for a choice of constant
C_{1}
large enough.
By Weyl’s inequality, combined with the uniform sparse-spectrum event in Lemma
6
, we have, simultaneously for all relevant sets
J
,

\widehat{M}\leq M+CM\sqrt{|T|\frac{\log\big(\frac{ep}{|T|}\big)}{{n}}}.

This yields

{|T|\leq C_{1}M^{2}s_{u}+C_{1}M^{2}s_{u}\sqrt{|T|\frac{\log\big(\frac{ep}{|T|}\big)}{n}}.}

Solving this one-dimensional inequality on the same sparse-spectrum event gives

|T|\leq CM^{2}s_{u},

provided
c_{0}
in
s_{u}\log(ep/s_{u})/n\leq c_{0}
is sufficiently small. Using that
x/\log(ep/x)
is increasing on the relevant range, if
|T|>2C_{1}M^{2}s_{u}
, then the preceding display implies

|T|\leq 4C_{1}^{2}M^{4}s_{u}^{2}\frac{\log(ep/|T|)}{n},

which contradicts
|T|>CM^{2}s_{u}
for a sufficiently large constant
C
under the same smallness assumption.
Therefore, on the same event as above, we obtain:

|{\rm{supp}}{\widehat{\mathbf{B}}}|\lesssim{M^{2}}s_{u}.

(45)

∎

D.3
Proof of Theorem
4

Proof.

The first part of this argument follows the exact same path as the proof of Theorem
2
.
Let

\mathbf{V}_{0}:=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{1/2}\mathbf{V},\qquad\mathbf{B}^{*}:=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{V}_{0}^{\top},\qquad S:={\rm{supp}}(\mathbf{B}^{*})={\rm{supp}}(\mathbf{U}).

Then
\mathbf{V}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{0}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
and

\operatorname{col}(\mathbf{B}^{*})=\operatorname{col}(\mathbf{U}),\qquad\operatorname{row}(\mathbf{B}^{*})=\operatorname{col}(\mathbf{V}_{0}).

Let

\mathbf{B}^{*}=\mathbf{U}_{B}\mathbf{D}_{B}\mathbf{V}_{B}^{\top}

be the compact singular value decomposition of
\mathbf{B}^{*}
, where

\mathbf{U}_{B}
and
\mathbf{V}_{B}
have orthonormal columns. Since

\mathbf{V}_{0}
is orthonormal and spans the same subspace as
\mathbf{V}_{B}
, there exists

\mathbf{Q}_{Y}\in\mathcal{O}_{r}
such that

\mathbf{V}_{B}=\mathbf{V}_{0}\mathbf{Q}_{Y}.

Similarly, define

\mathbf{U}_{\diamond}:=\mathbf{U}_{B}(\mathbf{U}_{B}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\mathbf{U}_{B})^{-1/2}.

Then
\mathbf{U}_{\diamond}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\mathbf{U}_{\diamond}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
and

\operatorname{col}(\mathbf{U}_{\diamond})=\operatorname{col}(\mathbf{U})
. Since

\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\mathbf{U}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
, there exists

\mathbf{Q}_{X}\in\mathcal{O}_{r}
such that

\mathbf{U}_{\diamond}=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{Q}_{X}.

(46)

Let
\mathbf{\Delta}:=\widehat{\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{B}^{*}
. We first make the probability bookkeeping explicit. Fix the target constant
C^{\prime}>0
in the theorem statement and apply the concentration bounds below with an auxiliary constant
C_{\star}>C^{\prime}
chosen sufficiently large; after the final union bound we relabel the resulting exponent by
C^{\prime}
.

Let
{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}
denote the event:

\begin{split}{\mathcal{A}_{Y}=\Big\{\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{Y}-{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{Y}\right\|_{op}^{2}\leq\frac{CM^{2}q}{n},\quad\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{op}\leq 2\sqrt{M},\quad\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{op}\leq CM^{2}\sqrt{\frac{q}{n}}\Big\}.}\end{split}

(47)

By Lemma
5
and the identity

\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}=\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-1/2}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{1/2}-\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{1/2})\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2},

we have
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{Y}^{c})\leq\exp\{-C_{\star}q\}
, provided
c_{0}
is sufficiently small.

Let
\mathcal{G}
denote the event:

\mathcal{G}=\Big\{\Big\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{\Delta}\Big\|_{F}^{2}\leq C_{1}M^{5}s_{u}\frac{q+\log p}{n},\quad\|\widehat{\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{B}^{*}\|_{F}^{2}\leq C_{2}M^{6}s_{u}\frac{q+\log p}{n},\quad|{\rm{supp}}(\widehat{\mathbf{B}})|\leq C_{J}M^{2}s_{u}\Big\}.

By Theorem
3
, applied with exponent
C_{\star}
,

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{G}^{c})\leq\exp\{-C_{\star}q\}+\exp\left\{-C_{\star}s_{u}\log\left(\frac{ep}{s_{u}}\right)\right\}+\exp\{-C_{\star}(q+\log p)\}+\exp\{-C_{\star}(r+\log p)\}.

Let
s_{J}:=\lceil(C_{J}M^{2}+1)s_{u}\rceil
and define the uniform sparse-covariance event

\mathcal{H}_{X}=\left\{\max_{J\subset[p]:|J|\leq s_{J}}\left\|[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}]_{JJ}\right\|_{op}\leq C_{4}M^{2}\sqrt{\frac{s_{u}\log(ep/s_{u})}{n}}\right\}.

By Lemma
5
and a union bound over all subsets of size at most
s_{J}
,

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{H}_{X}^{c})\leq\exp\{-C_{\star}s_{J}\log(ep/s_{J})\}\leq\exp\left\{-C_{\star}^{\prime}s_{u}\log\left(\frac{ep}{s_{u}}\right)\right\},

where the last inequality uses that
M
is treated as an absolute constant. Hence, for

\mathcal{E}:=\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{A}_{Y}\cap\mathcal{H}_{X},

choosing
C_{\star}
large enough and decreasing constants if necessary gives

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\geq 1-\exp\{-C^{\prime}q\}-\exp\left\{-C^{\prime}s_{u}\log\left(\frac{ep}{s_{u}}\right)\right\}-\exp\{-C^{\prime}(q+\log p)\}-\exp\{-C^{\prime}(r+\log p)\}.

All bounds below are proved on the event
\mathcal{E}
.

Step 1: perturbation of the singular subspaces of
\widehat{\mathbf{B}}
.

By Theorem
3
, on the event
\mathcal{E}
,

\|\widehat{\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{B}^{*}\|_{F}\leq C_{1}M^{3}\sqrt{s_{u}\frac{q+\log p}{n}},\qquad|{\rm{supp}}(\widehat{\mathbf{B}})|\lesssim M^{2}s_{u}.

(48)

Moreover,

\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{B}^{*})=\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{U}\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{V}_{0}^{\top})\geq\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{U})\lambda_{r}\geq\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{M}},

because
\mathbf{V}_{0}
has orthonormal columns and

\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\mathbf{U}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
with

\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\|_{op}\leq M
implies

\mathbf{I}_{r}=\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\mathbf{U}\preceq M\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{U}
, hence

\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{U})\geq M^{-1/2}
. For
c_{0}
sufficiently small, the right-hand side of
(
48
) is smaller than a fixed fraction of

\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{B}^{*})
. Therefore Wedin’s theorem (Lemma
8
) applied directly to

\widehat{\mathbf{B}}
and
\mathbf{B}^{*}
gives orthogonal matrices

\mathbf{O}_{X},\mathbf{O}_{Y}\in\mathcal{O}_{r}
such that

\|\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{\widehat{B}}-\mathbf{U}_{B}\mathbf{O}_{X}\|_{F}\vee\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\widehat{B}}-\mathbf{V}_{B}\mathbf{O}_{Y}\|_{F}\leq C_{2}\frac{M^{7/2}}{\lambda}\sqrt{rs_{u}\frac{q+\log p}{n}}.

(49)

Step 2: Error bound on
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}
.

Since
\mathbf{V}_{B}=\mathbf{V}_{0}\mathbf{Q}_{Y}
and

\mathbf{V}_{0}=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{1/2}\mathbf{V}
,

\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}\mathbf{V}_{B}=\mathbf{V}\mathbf{Q}_{Y}.

Furthermore, on
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
,

\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{op}\leq 2\sqrt{M},\qquad\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{op}\leq CM^{2}\sqrt{\frac{q}{n}}.

(50)

Thus, using (
49
),

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}-\mathbf{V}\mathbf{Q}_{Y}\mathbf{O}_{Y}\|_{F}

\displaystyle=\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-1/2}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\widehat{B}}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}\mathbf{V}_{B}\mathbf{O}_{Y}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-1/2}\|_{op}\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\widehat{B}}-\mathbf{V}_{B}\mathbf{O}_{Y}\|_{F}+\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}\|_{op}\|\mathbf{V}_{B}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq{2\sqrt{M}\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\widehat{B}}-\mathbf{V}_{B}\mathbf{O}_{Y}\|_{F}+CM^{2}\sqrt{\frac{q}{n}}\,\|\mathbf{V}_{B}\|_{F}}

\displaystyle\leq{C_{3}\frac{M^{11/2}}{\lambda}\sqrt{rs_{u}\frac{q+\log p}{n}}.}

Taking
\mathbf{O}=\mathbf{Q}_{Y}\mathbf{O}_{Y}\in\mathcal{O}_{r}
proves the desired bound for

\widehat{\mathbf{V}}
on the event
\mathcal{E}
.

Step 3: Error bound on
\widehat{\mathbf{U}}
.

Let

\widehat{\mathbf{A}}:=\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{\widehat{B}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{\widehat{B}},\qquad\mathbf{A}:=\mathbf{U}_{B}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\mathbf{U}_{B}.

The matrix
\mathbf{A}
is positive definite, with eigenvalues in
[1/M,M]
. Let

J:={\rm{supp}}(\widehat{\mathbf{B}})\cup S
. On the event
\mathcal{E}
,

|J|\leq s_{J}
. Since the columns of
\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{\widehat{B}}
are supported on

{\rm{supp}}(\widehat{\mathbf{B}})
and the columns of
\mathbf{U}_{B}
are supported on
S
, the event
\mathcal{H}_{X}
gives

\|[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}]_{JJ}\|_{op}\leq C_{4}M^{2}\sqrt{\frac{s_{u}\log(ep/s_{u})}{n}}.

Together with (
49
) and

\left\|[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{JJ}\right\|_{op}\leq CM
on
\mathcal{E}
, this implies

\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{O}_{X}^{\top}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{O}_{X}\|_{F}\leq{C_{5}\frac{M^{9/2}}{\lambda}}\sqrt{rs_{u}\frac{q+\log p}{n}}.

(51)

Let

\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}:=\mathbf{O}_{X}^{\top}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{O}_{X}.

Since the eigenvalues of
\mathbf{A}
lie in
[1/M,M]
, the same is true for

\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}
. Moreover, by (
51
)
and the smallness assumption on
c_{0}
, we have

\lambda_{\min}(\widehat{\mathbf{A}})\geq\frac{1}{2M},\qquad\lambda_{\min}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}})\geq\frac{1}{M}.

In particular, for
c_{0}
sufficiently small,
\widehat{\mathbf{A}}
is therefore positive definite.
We now use the standard inverse-square-root perturbation bound for positive definite
matrices: if
\mathbf{H}_{1},\mathbf{H}_{2}\succ 0
and

\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{H}_{1})\wedge\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{H}_{2})\geq a,

then

\|\mathbf{H}_{1}^{-1/2}-\mathbf{H}_{2}^{-1/2}\|_{F}\leq\frac{1}{2a^{3/2}}\|\mathbf{H}_{1}-\mathbf{H}_{2}\|_{F}.

For completeness, this follows from the resolvent representation

\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-1/2}(\mathbf{H}+t\mathbf{I})^{-1}\,dt,

which, using the identity
\mathbf{A}^{-1}-\mathbf{B}^{-1}=\mathbf{A}^{-1}(\mathbf{B}-\mathbf{A})\mathbf{B}^{-1}
applied to
\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{H}_{1}+t\mathbf{I}
and
\mathbf{B}=\mathbf{H}_{2}+t\mathbf{I}
, gives

\mathbf{H}_{1}^{-1/2}-\mathbf{H}_{2}^{-1/2}=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-1/2}(\mathbf{H}_{1}+t\mathbf{I})^{-1}(\mathbf{H}_{2}-\mathbf{H}_{1})(\mathbf{H}_{2}+t\mathbf{I})^{-1}\,dt.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{H}_{1}^{-1/2}-\mathbf{H}_{2}^{-1/2}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\pi}\|\mathbf{H}_{1}-\mathbf{H}_{2}\|_{F}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{t^{-1/2}}{(a+t)^{2}}\,dt

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2a^{3/2}}\|\mathbf{H}_{1}-\mathbf{H}_{2}\|_{F}.

Applying this bound with

\mathbf{H}_{1}=\widehat{\mathbf{A}}
,

\mathbf{H}_{2}=\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}
, and
a=1/(2M)
yields

\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}^{-1/2}-\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1/2}\|_{F}\leq CM^{3/2}\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}-\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\|_{F}.

Finally, by orthogonal invariance of the functional calculus,

\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1/2}=(\mathbf{O}_{X}^{\top}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{O}_{X})^{-1/2}=\mathbf{O}_{X}^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{-1/2}\mathbf{O}_{X}.

Hence

\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}^{-1/2}-\mathbf{O}_{X}^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{-1/2}\mathbf{O}_{X}\|_{F}\leq CM^{3/2}\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{O}_{X}^{\top}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{O}_{X}\|_{F}.

Combining this with (
51
) gives

\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}^{-1/2}-\mathbf{O}_{X}^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{-1/2}\mathbf{O}_{X}\|_{F}\leq C_{6}\frac{M^{6}}{\lambda}\sqrt{rs_{u}\frac{q+\log p}{n}},

provided that (
51
) is stated with the
corresponding
M^{9/2}/\lambda
rate.

By the
Lipschitz continuity of the inverse-square-root map on matrices whose eigenvalues are
bounded below by
1/(2M)
,

\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}^{-1/2}-\mathbf{O}_{X}^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{-1/2}\mathbf{O}_{X}\|_{F}\leq C_{6}\frac{M^{6}}{\lambda}\sqrt{rs_{u}\frac{q+\log p}{n}}.

(52)

Now

\widehat{\mathbf{U}}=\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{\widehat{B}}\widehat{\mathbf{A}}^{-1/2},\qquad\mathbf{U}_{\diamond}=\mathbf{U}_{B}\mathbf{A}^{-1/2}.

Using (
49
) and (
52
),

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mathbf{U}}-\mathbf{U}_{\diamond}\mathbf{O}_{X}\|_{F}

\displaystyle=\|\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{\widehat{B}}\widehat{\mathbf{A}}^{-1/2}-\mathbf{U}_{B}\mathbf{A}^{-1/2}\mathbf{O}_{X}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{\widehat{B}}-\mathbf{U}_{B}\mathbf{O}_{X}\|_{F}\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}^{-1/2}\|_{op}+\|\mathbf{U}_{B}\|_{op}\|\mathbf{O}_{X}\widehat{\mathbf{A}}^{-1/2}-\mathbf{A}^{-1/2}\mathbf{O}_{X}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq C_{7}\frac{M^{6}}{\lambda}\sqrt{rs_{u}\frac{q+\log p}{n}}.

Finally, by (
46
),

\|\widehat{\mathbf{U}}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{Q}_{X}\mathbf{O}_{X}\|_{F}\leq C_{7}\frac{M^{6}}{\lambda}\sqrt{rs_{u}\frac{q+\log p}{n}}.

The last line follows from
\|U_{B}\|_{op}=1
.
Taking
\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}=\mathbf{Q}_{X}\mathbf{O}_{X}\in\mathcal{O}_{r}
proves the bound for

\widehat{\mathbf{U}}
.
Since all estimates above hold on
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{E}
has the probability lower bound displayed above, the theorem follows.
This completes the proof.

∎

Lemma 8

(Wedin’s sin–
\Theta
theorem, rank-
r
signal form)
.

Let
\mathbf{A},\widehat{\mathbf{A}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times q}
, and suppose

\mathbf{A}
has rank
r
with compact singular value decomposition

\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{V}^{\top},\qquad\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{A})>0,

where
\mathbf{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times r}
and

\mathbf{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{q\times r}
have orthonormal columns. Let

\widehat{\mathbf{A}}=\widehat{\mathbf{U}}\widehat{\mathbf{D}}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}+\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{\perp}

be a singular value decomposition of
\widehat{\mathbf{A}}
, where

\widehat{\mathbf{U}}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}
contain the leading
r
left and
right singular vectors. Set

\mathbf{E}=\widehat{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{A}.

If

\|\mathbf{E}\|_{op}\leq\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{A}),

then

\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{\mathbf{U}},\mathbf{U})\|_{F}\vee\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{\mathbf{V}},\mathbf{V})\|_{F}\leq C_{W}\frac{\|\mathbf{E}\|_{F}}{\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{A})},

for a universal numerical constant
C_{W}
.
Consequently, there exist orthogonal matrices

\mathbf{O}_{U},\mathbf{O}_{V}\in\mathcal{O}_{r}
such that

\|\widehat{\mathbf{U}}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{O}_{U}\|_{F}\vee\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}-\mathbf{V}\mathbf{O}_{V}\|_{F}\leq C_{W}^{\prime}\frac{\|\mathbf{E}\|_{F}}{\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{A})},

where
C_{W}^{\prime}
is another universal numerical constant.

Proof.

Let

\sigma:=\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{A})>0,\qquad\mathbf{E}=\widehat{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{A}.

Let

\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{V}^{\top}

be the compact singular value decomposition of
\mathbf{A}
, and extend

\mathbf{U}
and
\mathbf{V}
to orthogonal matrices

[\mathbf{U},\mathbf{U}_{\perp}]
and
[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{V}_{\perp}]
.
Then

\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{\mathbf{U}},\mathbf{U})\|_{F}=\|\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{U}}\|_{F},\qquad\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{\mathbf{V}},\mathbf{V})\|_{F}=\|\mathbf{V}_{\perp}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}\|_{F}.

By Weyl’s inequality,

\sigma_{r}(\widehat{\mathbf{A}})\geq\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{A})-\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{A}\|_{op}\geq\frac{\sigma}{2}.

Thus the leading
r
singular values collected in

\widehat{\mathbf{D}}
are bounded below by
\sigma/2
, and

\widehat{\mathbf{D}}
is invertible with

\|\widehat{\mathbf{D}}^{-1}\|_{op}\leq\frac{2}{\sigma}.

We first bound the left singular subspace. Since

\widehat{\mathbf{A}}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}=\widehat{\mathbf{U}}\widehat{\mathbf{D}},

we have

\mathbf{A}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}=\widehat{\mathbf{U}}\widehat{\mathbf{D}}-\mathbf{E}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}.

Because the columns of
\mathbf{A}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}
lie in

\operatorname{col}(\mathbf{U})
, multiplying by
\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}
gives

\mathbf{0}=\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{U}}\widehat{\mathbf{D}}-\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{E}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}.

Therefore,

\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{U}}=\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{E}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}\widehat{\mathbf{D}}^{-1}.

Hence

\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{\mathbf{U}},\mathbf{U})\|_{F}=\|\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{U}}\|_{F}\leq\|\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{E}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}\|_{F}\|\widehat{\mathbf{D}}^{-1}\|_{op}\leq\frac{2\|\mathbf{E}\|_{F}}{\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{A})}.

The right singular subspace is handled in the same way. Since

\widehat{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{U}}=\widehat{\mathbf{V}}\widehat{\mathbf{D}},

we have

\mathbf{A}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{U}}=\widehat{\mathbf{V}}\widehat{\mathbf{D}}-\mathbf{E}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{U}}.

The columns of
\mathbf{A}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{U}}
lie in

\operatorname{col}(\mathbf{V})
, so multiplying by
\mathbf{V}_{\perp}^{\top}
yields

\mathbf{0}=\mathbf{V}_{\perp}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}\widehat{\mathbf{D}}-\mathbf{V}_{\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{E}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{U}}.

Thus

\mathbf{V}_{\perp}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}=\mathbf{V}_{\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{E}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{U}}\widehat{\mathbf{D}}^{-1},

and consequently

\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{\mathbf{V}},\mathbf{V})\|_{F}=\|\mathbf{V}_{\perp}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}\|_{F}\leq\frac{2\|\mathbf{E}\|_{F}}{\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{A})}.

Combining the two displays gives

\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{\mathbf{U}},\mathbf{U})\|_{F}\vee\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{\mathbf{V}},\mathbf{V})\|_{F}\leq 2\frac{\|\mathbf{E}\|_{F}}{\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{A})}.

Thus the first conclusion holds with, for example,
C_{W}=2
.

It remains to convert the sin–
\Theta
bound into a bound after orthogonal
alignment. For matrices with orthonormal columns, the Procrustes inequality gives

\min_{\mathbf{O}\in\mathcal{O}_{r}}\|\widehat{\mathbf{U}}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{O}\|_{F}\leq\sqrt{2}\,\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{\mathbf{U}},\mathbf{U})\|_{F},

and similarly

\min_{\mathbf{O}\in\mathcal{O}_{r}}\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}-\mathbf{V}\mathbf{O}\|_{F}\leq\sqrt{2}\,\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{\mathbf{V}},\mathbf{V})\|_{F}.

Indeed, if the singular values of

\mathbf{U}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{U}}
are

\cos\theta_{1},\ldots,\cos\theta_{r}
, then

\min_{\mathbf{O}\in\mathcal{O}_{r}}\|\widehat{\mathbf{U}}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{O}\|_{F}^{2}=2\sum_{j=1}^{r}(1-\cos\theta_{j})\leq 2\sum_{j=1}^{r}\sin^{2}\theta_{j}.

The same argument applies to
\mathbf{V}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}
.

Therefore, there exist

\mathbf{O}_{U},\mathbf{O}_{V}\in\mathcal{O}_{r}
such that

\|\widehat{\mathbf{U}}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{O}_{U}\|_{F}\vee\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}-\mathbf{V}\mathbf{O}_{V}\|_{F}\leq 2\sqrt{2}\,\frac{\|\mathbf{E}\|_{F}}{\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{A})}.

Thus the second conclusion holds with, for example,

C_{W}^{\prime}=2\sqrt{2}
.
∎

Appendix E
Proofs: group-sparse CCAR
3

We first introduce notation for group supports. Let

\mathcal{G}=\{g_{1},\ldots,g_{K}\}

be a non-overlapping partition of
[p]
, and write

d_{g}=|g|,\qquad d_{\min}=\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}}d_{g},\qquad d_{\max}=\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}}d_{g},\qquad\eta=\frac{d_{\max}}{d_{\min}}.

For a collection of groups
\mathcal{H}\subseteq\mathcal{G}
, define

T(\mathcal{H})=\bigcup_{g\in\mathcal{H}}g,\qquad d(\mathcal{H})=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{H}}d_{g}.

For a matrix
\mathbf{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times q}
, let

\operatorname{supp}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbf{B})=\{g\in\mathcal{G}:\|\mathbf{B}_{g}\|_{F}>0\},

where
\mathbf{B}_{g}
denotes the submatrix of
\mathbf{B}
formed by the rows indexed by
g
. We use the weighted group norm

\|\mathbf{B}\|_{\mathcal{G},21}=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\sqrt{d_{g}}\,\|\mathbf{B}_{g}\|_{F}.

Let

\mathbf{B}^{*}=\mathbf{U}{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\mathbf{V}_{0}^{\top},\qquad\mathbf{V}_{0}=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{1/2}\mathbf{V},

and define the true active group set

\mathcal{A}=\operatorname{supp}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbf{B}^{*})=\{g\in\mathcal{G}:\|\mathbf{B}_{g}^{*}\|_{F}>0\}.

Let

m_{u}=|\mathcal{A}|,\qquad s_{u}=d(\mathcal{A})=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{A}}d_{g}.

Note that
m_{u}\leq s_{u}/d_{\min}
. Define the group rate

R_{\mathcal{G}}=\frac{s_{u}q+(s_{u}/d_{\min})\log K}{n}.

Theorem 9

(Group-regularized regression error with support control)
.

Consider the group-penalized estimator

\widehat{\mathbf{B}}=\operatorname*{argmin}_{\mathbf{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times q}}\frac{1}{n}\|\mathbf{Y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{B}\|_{F}^{2}+\rho\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\sqrt{d_{g}}\,\|\mathbf{B}_{g}\|_{F}.

Assume that
R_{\mathcal{G}}\leq c_{0}
for a sufficiently small constant
c_{0}\in(0,1)
.
Assume also that
\eta=d_{\max}/d_{\min}
is bounded by an absolute constant; otherwise the constants below depend on
\eta
.
Let

\mathbf{\Delta}=\widehat{\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{B}^{*}.

Choose

\rho\geq C_{u}M^{2}\left(\sqrt{\frac{q}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log K}{d_{\min}n}}\right)

for a sufficiently large constant
C_{u}
. Then, for any
C^{\prime}>0
, with a probability of at least

1-\exp(-C^{\prime}q)-\exp\{-C^{\prime}(s_{u}/d_{\min})\log(eKd_{\min}/s_{u})\}-\exp\{-C^{\prime}(q+\log K)\}-\exp\{-C^{\prime}(r+\log K)\},

there exist constants
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}>0
, depending only on
C^{\prime}
,
c_{0}
, and the bounded group-size ratio, such that

\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}^{1/2}\mathbf{\Delta}\right\|_{F}^{2}\leq C_{1}M^{5}R_{\mathcal{G}},

and

\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{F}^{2}\leq C_{2}M^{6}R_{\mathcal{G}}.

Moreover, with the same probability, the selected group support satisfies

d(\widehat{\mathcal{A}})=\sum_{g\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}d_{g}\leq C_{3}M^{2}s_{u},\qquad|\widehat{\mathcal{A}}|\leq C_{3}M^{2}\frac{s_{u}}{d_{\min}},

where

\widehat{\mathcal{A}}=\operatorname{supp}_{\mathcal{G}}(\widehat{\mathbf{B}}).

Proof.

The proof follows the same regression argument as in the sparse case, but the cone and peeling steps are performed at the group level.
Let

\mathbf{Z}=\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{XY}\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}\mathbf{B}^{*}.

We work on the group score event

\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{score}}=\left\{\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}_{g}\|_{F}}{\sqrt{d_{g}}}\leq\frac{\rho}{4}\right\}.

We first verify the group score event. Recall that

\mathbf{Z}=\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{XY}\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}\mathbf{B}^{*},\qquad\mathbf{B}^{*}=\mathbf{U}{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\mathbf{V}_{0}^{\top},\qquad\mathbf{V}_{0}=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{1/2}\mathbf{V}.

Since

\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\mathbf{B}^{*}=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\mathbf{U}{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{1/2}=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{XY}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2},

we may decompose, for every group
g\in\mathcal{G}
,

\mathbf{Z}_{g}=\underbrace{\big[(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{XY}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{XY})\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-1/2}\big]_{g}}_{\mathbf{A}_{g}}+\underbrace{\big[\mathbf{\Sigma}_{XY}(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2})\big]_{g}}_{\mathbf{B}_{g}}-\underbrace{\big[(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X})\mathbf{B}^{*}\big]_{g}}_{\mathbf{C}_{g}}.

We work on the covariance event

\mathcal{E}_{Y}=\left\{\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{1/2}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{1/2}\|_{\text{op}}\leq CM\sqrt{\frac{q}{n}},\quad\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-1/2}\|_{\text{op}}\leq 2\sqrt{M}\right\}.

By Lemma
5
,

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{Y}^{c})\leq\exp(-C^{\prime}q),

provided
q/n\leq c_{0}
and
c_{0}
is sufficiently small. On
\mathcal{E}_{Y}
,

\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{1/2}\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-1/2}\|_{\text{op}}\leq CM,

and

\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{1/2}\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\mathbf{I}_{q}\|_{\text{op}}=\|(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{1/2}-\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{1/2})\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-1/2}\|_{\text{op}}\leq CM^{3/2}\sqrt{\frac{q}{n}}.

We now bound the three terms in
(E.0)
.

First, set

\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}\mathbf{Y}_{i}.

For a fixed group
g
, and for any matrix

\mathbf{H}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{g}\times q}
with
\|\mathbf{H}\|_{F}=1
,
the random variables

\xi_{i}(\mathbf{H})=\left\langle\mathbf{X}_{i,g}\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{X}_{i,g}\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}^{\top}),\mathbf{H}\right\rangle

are centered sub-exponential with

\|\xi_{i}(\mathbf{H})\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq CM.

Indeed,
(\mathbf{X}_{i,g},\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i})
is jointly Gaussian and its covariance operator norm is bounded by
CM
. Bernstein’s inequality, followed by a
1/4
-net argument over the Frobenius unit sphere in

\mathbb{R}^{d_{g}\times q}
, gives, for every
t>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left[\left\|\big[(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{XY}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{XY})\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}\big]_{g}\right\|_{F}>CM\sqrt{\frac{d_{g}q+t}{n}}\right]\leq\exp\{-c(d_{g}q+t)\},

where the linear Bernstein term is absorbed under the small-rate condition. Taking

t=\log K
and union-bounding over
g\in\mathcal{G}
gives

\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\frac{\left\|\big[(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{XY}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{XY})\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}\big]_{g}\right\|_{F}}{\sqrt{d_{g}}}\leq CM\left(\sqrt{\frac{q}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log K}{d_{\min}n}}\right)

with probability at least

1-\exp\{-C^{\prime}(q+\log K)\}.

Combining
(E.0c)
with

\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{1/2}\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-1/2}\|_{\text{op}}\leq CM
,
we obtain

\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\frac{\|\mathbf{A}_{g}\|_{F}}{\sqrt{d_{g}}}\leq CM^{2}\left(\sqrt{\frac{q}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log K}{d_{\min}n}}\right).

Second, for the term
\mathbf{B}_{g}
, note that

\mathbf{B}_{g}=\big[\mathbf{\Sigma}_{XY}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}\big]_{g}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{1/2}\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\mathbf{I}_{q}\right).

Moreover,

\frac{\|[\mathbf{\Sigma}_{XY}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}]_{g}\|_{F}}{\sqrt{d_{g}}}\leq\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{XY}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}\|_{\text{op}}=\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\mathbf{U}{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\mathbf{V}_{0}^{\top}\|_{\text{op}}\leq\sqrt{M}.

Therefore, using
(E.0a)
,

\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\frac{\|\mathbf{B}_{g}\|_{F}}{\sqrt{d_{g}}}\leq CM^{2}\sqrt{\frac{q}{n}}.

Third, for the term
\mathbf{C}_{g}
, since

\mathbf{B}^{*}=\mathbf{U}{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\mathbf{V}_{0}^{\top}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\left\|{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\mathbf{V}_{0}^{\top}\right\|_{op}\leq 1,

we have

\|\mathbf{C}_{g}\|_{F}\leq\left\|\big[(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X})\mathbf{U}\big]_{g}\right\|_{F}.

For a fixed group
g
, and for any

\mathbf{H}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{g}\times r}
with
\|\mathbf{H}\|_{F}=1
,
the variables

\zeta_{i}(\mathbf{H})=\left\langle\mathbf{X}_{i,g}(\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i})^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\{\mathbf{X}_{i,g}(\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i})^{\top}\},\mathbf{H}\right\rangle

are centered sub-exponential with

\|\zeta_{i}(\mathbf{H})\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq CM,

because
(\mathbf{X}_{i,g},\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i})
is jointly Gaussian, the covariance of

\mathbf{X}_{i,g}
has operator norm at most
M
, and

\operatorname{Cov}(\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i})=\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\mathbf{U}=I_{r}.

Another Bernstein plus net argument gives, for every
t>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left[\left\|\big[(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X})\mathbf{U}\big]_{g}\right\|_{F}>CM\sqrt{\frac{d_{g}r+t}{n}}\right]\leq\exp\{-c(d_{g}r+t)\}.

Taking
t=\log K
, union-bounding over the
K
groups, and using
r\leq q
, we get

\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\frac{\|\mathbf{C}_{g}\|_{F}}{\sqrt{d_{g}}}\leq CM\left(\sqrt{\frac{r}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log K}{d_{\min}n}}\right)\leq CM^{2}\left(\sqrt{\frac{q}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log K}{d_{\min}n}}\right)

with probability at least

1-\exp\{-C^{\prime}(r+\log K)\}.

Combining
(E.0d)
,
(E.0e)
, and
(E.0g)
, we conclude that

\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}_{g}\|_{F}}{\sqrt{d_{g}}}\leq CM^{2}\left(\sqrt{\frac{q}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log K}{d_{\min}n}}\right)

with probability at least

1-\exp(-C^{\prime}q)-\exp\{-C^{\prime}(q+\log K)\}-\exp\{-C^{\prime}(r+\log K)\}.

Thus, choosing

\rho\geq C_{\rho}M^{2}\left(\sqrt{\frac{q}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log K}{d_{\min}n}}\right)

with
C_{\rho}
sufficiently large ensures the group score event

\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{score}}=\left\{\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}_{g}\|_{F}}{\sqrt{d_{g}}}\leq\frac{\rho}{4}\right\}.

Thus, the stated choice of
\rho
ensures
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{score}}
with high probability.

We also work on a uniform group-sparse covariance event. Specifically, for all collections

\mathcal{\mathbf{}}{H}\subseteq\mathcal{G}
,

\left\|[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}]_{T(\mathcal{H}),T(\mathcal{H})}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq CM\sqrt{\frac{d(\mathcal{H})+|\mathcal{H}|\log(eK/|\mathcal{H}|)}{n}}.

This follows by applying the usual sub-Gaussian sample-covariance bound to each fixed group union and union-bounding over group subsets. On the group-sparse sizes used below, the right-hand side is small because
R_{\mathcal{G}}\leq c_{0}
. Hence, for all such group unions,

\lambda_{\min}\left([\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{T(\mathcal{H}),T(\mathcal{H})}\right)\geq\frac{1}{2M},\qquad\lambda_{\max}\left([\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]_{T(\mathcal{H}),T(\mathcal{H})}\right)\leq 2M,

provided
c_{0}
is small enough.

We now prove the regression error bound.
By optimality of
\widehat{\mathbf{B}}
,

\frac{1}{n}\|\mathbf{Y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}\widehat{\mathbf{B}}\|_{F}^{2}+\rho\|\widehat{\mathbf{B}}\|_{\mathcal{G},21}\leq\frac{1}{n}\|\mathbf{Y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{B}^{*}\|_{F}^{2}+\rho\|\mathbf{B}^{*}\|_{\mathcal{G},21}.

Writing
\mathbf{\Delta}=\widehat{\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{B}^{*}
, this gives
